Remove the old (old_score, player_id) entry from the min-heap\n2. Add the new (new_score, player_id) entry\n3. The final Top-K leaderboard is written\\nto an output JSON file sorted by score in descending order.\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python leaderboard.py --input <input_file> --output <output_file> --k <k>\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport heapq\\nimport json\\nimport sys\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nfrom typing import List, Optional, Tuple\\n\\nfrom typing_extensions import override\\n\\nfrom bytewax._bytewax import run_main\\nfrom bytewax.connectors.files import FileSource\\nfrom bytewax.dataflow import Dataflow\\nfrom bytewax.operators import (\\n    input,\\n    key_on,\\n    map,\\n    output,\\n    stateful_map,\\n)\\nfrom bytewax.outputs import DynamicSink, StatelessSinkPartition\\n\\n\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Stateful mapper: track the highest score seen for each player.\\n#\\n# Input value: a dict {\\\"player_id\\\": str, \\\"score\\\": number}.\\n# State: float | None  (previous highest score for this player).\\n# Output value: a dict {\\\"player_id\\\": str, \\\"score\\\": <current max>} for\\n# downstream consumption.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\ndef track_max(state: Optional[float], event: dict) -> Tuple[float, dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Update the per-player max score and emit the current max.\\n\\n    The output dict is always emitted so the downstream top-K tracker sees\\n    every player's current maximum. Redundant emissions for the same max\\n    value are safely idempotent in the top-K tracker.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    new_score = event[\\\"score\\\"]\\n    if state is None:\\n        new_max = new_score\\n    elif new_score > state:\\n        new_max = new_score\\n    else:\\n        new_max = state\\n\\n    output_event = {\\n        \\\"player_id\\\": event[\\\"player_id\\\"],\\n        \\\"score\\\": new_max,\\n    }\\n    return (new_max, output_event)\\n\\n\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Stateful mapper: maintain the global Top-K players using a min-heap.\\n#\\n# State: list of (score, player_id) tuples maintained as a max-heap using\\n#        ``heapq`` (which is a min-heap, so we store negated scores to\\n#        emulate max semantics on pop). The list is bounded to K entries.\\n# Input value: dict {\\\"player_id\\\": str, \\\"score\\\": number}  (player's current max).\\n# Output value: list of dicts {\\\"player_id\\\": str, \\\"score\\\": number} sorted by\\n#               score descending — the current Top-K view.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nclass _TopKState:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Wrapper around a max-heap bounded to K entries.\\n\\n    We store negated scores in a min-heap so that ``heapq.heappop`` always\\n    removes the *smallest* entry (i.e. the player outside the Top-K).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    __slots__ = (\\\"_heap\\\", \\\"_k\\\")\\n\\n    def __init__(self, k: int):\\n        # Heap entries: (-score, player_id). The 0-th tuple element drives\\n        # the heap ordering, so negating the score produces a max-heap via\\n        # Python's stdlib min-heap.\\n        self._heap: List[Tuple[float, str]] = []\\n        self._k = k\\n\\n    def update(self, player_id: str, score: float) -> None:\\n        # Remove any prior entry for this player so stale tuples don't\\n        # linger in the heap.\\n        self._heap = [(s, p) for (s, p) in self._heap if p != player_id]\\n        heapq.heapify(self._heap)\\n        # Insert the new score and trim to K.\\n        heapq.heappush(self._heap, (-float(score), player_id))\\n        while len(self._heap) > self._k:\\n            heapq.heappop(self._heap)\\n\\n    def snapshot(self) -> List[dict]:\\n        # Build a sorted (descending by score) view of the current heap.\\n        ordered = sorted(self._heap, key=lambda sp: sp[0])  # ascending by -score\\n        return [\\n            {\\\"player_id\\\": p, \\\"score\\\": -s}\\n            for (s, p) in ordered\\n        ]\\n\\n\\ndef _make_track_top_k(k: int):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Build a stateful_map mapper closure that knows the Top-K size.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def track_top_k(\\n        state: Optional[_TopKState], event: dict\\n    ) -> Tuple[_TopKState, List[dict]]:\\n        if state is None:\\n            state = _TopKState(k)\\n        state.update(event[\\\"player_id\\\"], event[\\\"score\\\"])\\n        snapshot = state.snapshot()\\n        return (state, snapshot)\\n\\n    return track_top_k\\n\\n\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Custom sink that captures the very last emitted item — the final Top-K.\\n# Using a DynamicSink keeps everything inside the Bytewax execution model.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nclass _CaptureSinkPartition(StatelessSinkPartition):\\n    def __init__(self, owner: \\\"FinalCaptureSink\\\"):\\n        self._owner = owner\\n\\n    @override\\n    def write_batch(self, items: List[object]) -> None:\\n        # Items within a single batch arrive in order; we just want the\\n        # most-recent (i.e. last) one. After the dataflow completes, this\\n        # will be the final Top-K as it stood at EOF.\\n        if items:\\n            self._owner.value = items[-1]\\n\\n\\nclass FinalCaptureSink(DynamicSink[object]):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Sinks items into a Python attribute — ``value`` — for later use.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\\n        self.value: Optional[object] = None\\n\\n    @override\\n    def build(\\n        self, _step_id: str, _worker_index: int, _worker_count: int\\n    ) -> _CaptureSinkPartition:\\n        return _CaptureSinkPartition(self)\\n\\n\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Dataflow construction.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\ndef build_flow(k: int, sink: FinalCaptureSink) -> Dataflow:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Assemble the leaderboard dataflow.\\n\\n    The pipeline is:\\n\\n      FileSource (raw JSONL lines)\\n        -> map (JSON parse)\\n        -> key_on (player_id)\\n        -> stateful_map (per-player max score)\\n        -> map_value (drop the player_id from the value, keeping it in key)\\n        -> key_rm (recover (player_id, score) tuples)\\n        -> key_on (constant \\\"global\\\" key)  -- routes to single worker\\n        -> stateful_map (global Top-K maintenance via min-heap)\\n        -> output (FinalCaptureSink)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # ``input_path`` is supplied later via a closure; we read it from stdin\\n    # in the runtime harness via ``FileSource``. For building, we use a\\n    # placeholder filename because FileSource is lazy — the actual path is\\n    # patched in just before ``run_main`` is called.\\n    raise NotImplementedError(\\n        \\\"Use build_flow_for_path(path, k, sink) to construct the dataflow.\\\"\\n    )\\n\\n\\ndef build_flow_for_path(\\n    input_path: str, k: int, sink: FinalCaptureSink\\n) -> Dataflow:\\n    flow = Dataflow(\\\"leaderboard\\\")\\n    # 1. Read raw lines from the JSONL input file (lazy).\\n    lines = input(\\\"inp_lines\\\", flow, FileSource(input_path))\\n    # 2. Parse each line as JSON. Empty/blank lines are skipped.\\n    events = map(\\\"parse\\\", lines, _safe_json_loads)\\n    # 3. Key the stream by player_id so each player's score updates are\\n    #    routed to the same stateful worker.\\n    keyed = key_on(\\\"key_player\\\", events, lambda e: e[\\\"player_id\\\"])\\n    # 4. Per-player max-score tracking using stateful_map.\\n    max_scores: \\\"...\\\" = stateful_map(\\\"track_max\\\", keyed, track_max)\\n    # 5. Drop the player_id key off the stream so we can re-key to \\\"global\\\"\\n    #    while keeping the player_id information inside the value.\\n    pairs = max_scores.key_rm(\\\"rm_player_key\\\")\\n    # 6. Route every (player_id, score) pair to a single \\\"global\\\" key so\\n    #    a single stateful operator computes the global Top-K.\\n    global_stream = key_on(\\\"global_key\\\", pairs, lambda _item: \\\"global\\\")\\n    # 7. blocks until EOF.\\n    run_main(flow)\\n\\n    # Extract the captured final Top-K and normalise the output schema.\\n    if sink.value is None:\\n        leaderboard: List[dict] = []\\n    else:\\n        snapshot = sink.value  # already a list[dict]\\n        if not isinstance(snapshot, list):\\n            leaderboard = []\\n        else:\\n            # Sort descending by score, breaking ties stably on the dict order.\\n            leaderboard = sorted(\\n                snapshot,\\n                key=lambda d: float(d[\\\"score\\\"]),\\n                reverse=True,\\n            )\\n\\n    output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\n    with output_path.open(\\\"w\\\", encoding=\\\"utf-8\\\") as fh:\\n        json.dump(leaderboard, fh, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)\\n    return 0\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    sys.exit(main())\\n\"}"}}]},{"role":"tool","tool_call_id":"call_a3HHJ7Heqr92LDQRmm0O2vY0","content":"{\"success\":true}"},{"role":"assistant","content":"<think>\nNow let me test the implementation with sample data. Keep only top-K\n\nThis is tricky because removing an arbitrary element from a heap is O(n).\n\nApproach 1 (simpler, but works): Use a sorted list/dict of player_id -> score for ALL players, then take top-K at end. This violates the memory hint.\n\nApproach 2 (heap-based): Use a min-heap of size K, but lazy-delete stale entries.\n- Store min-heap of (score, player_id) entries.\n- On each update for player P with new_score:\n  - Push (new_score, P) to heap\n  - The stale (old_score, P) is still there, but will eventually be popped\n  - At output time, filter the heap to remove duplicates\n  - Or: when popping, check if it's stale\n\nThis is \"lazy deletion\" pattern. State maintains min-heap and a dict of \"latest scores\".\n\nActually wait - the requirement says \"Maintain a global state to track the top K players overall based on their highest scores.\" It says global state.\n\nLet me look at this differently. The MINIMUM requirement: track max score per player (stateful_map, keyed by player_id), and then aggregate to global top-K.\n\nThe challenge: when a player's max increases, we need to update the top-K. If the player was already in top-K, we update their entry. If not, they may enter top-K.\n\nApproach 3 (cleaner): Use stateful_map on global key, but maintain:\n- a) A dict {player_id: max_score} for ALL players (violates memory hint)\n- b) A sorted list of (score, player_id) for top-K (good)\n\nFor (b), we need to handle updates correctly.\n\nOK let me think about this. The simplest correct approach:\n\n```python\ndef track_top_k(state, item):\n    \"\"\"Maintain top-K via sorted list of size K.\"\"\"\n    if state is None:\n        state = []  # Sorted descending by score\n    \n    player_id, score = item\n    \n    # Remove old entry for this player (if any)\n    state = [(s, p) for s, p in state if p != player_id]\n    \n    # Add the new entry\n    state.append((score, player_id))\n    \n    # Sort descending and keep top K\n    state = sorted(state, key=lambda x: -x[0])[:k]\n    \n    return (state, state)\n```\n\nThis is O(k) for the filter + O(k log k) for sort = O(k log k) per update. Acceptable.\n\nActually wait, since `state` is already maintained in sorted order, we can do better:\n- Remove old (binary search for the player, then remove)\n- Insert new in sorted position (binary search + insert)\n- Truncate to k\n\nBut the simpler approach above is fine for correctness. The final Top-K leaderboard is written\\nto an output JSON file sorted by score in descending order.\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python leaderboard.py --input <input_file> --output <output_file> --k <k>\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport heapq\\nimport json\\nimport sys\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nfrom typing import List, Optional, Tuple\\n\\nfrom typing_extensions import override\\n\\nfrom bytewax._bytewax import run_main\\nfrom bytewax.connectors.files import FileSource\\nfrom bytewax.dataflow import Dataflow\\nfrom bytewax.operators import (\\n    input,\\n    key_on,\\n    map,\\n    output,\\n    stateful_map,\\n)\\nfrom bytewax.outputs import DynamicSink, StatelessSinkPartition\\n\\n\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Stateful mapper: track the highest score seen for each player.\\n#\\n# Input value: a dict {\\\"player_id\\\": str, \\\"score\\\": number}.\\n# State: float | None  (previous highest score for this player).\\n# Output value: a dict {\\\"player_id\\\": str, \\\"score\\\": <current max>} for\\n# downstream consumption.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\ndef track_max(state: Optional[float], event: dict) -> Tuple[float, dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Update the per-player max score and emit the current max.\\n\\n    The output dict is always emitted so the downstream top-K tracker sees\\n    every player's current maximum. Redundant emissions for the same max\\n    value are safely idempotent in the top-K tracker.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    new_score = event[\\\"score\\\"]\\n    if state is None:\\n        new_max = new_score\\n    elif new_score > state:\\n        new_max = new_score\\n    else:\\n        new_max = state\\n\\n    output_event = {\\n        \\\"player_id\\\": event[\\\"player_id\\\"],\\n        \\\"score\\\": new_max,\\n    }\\n    return (new_max, output_event)\\n\\n\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Stateful mapper: maintain the global Top-K players using a min-heap.\\n#\\n# State: list of (score, player_id) tuples maintained as a max-heap using\\n#        ``heapq`` (which is a min-heap, so we store negated scores to\\n#        emulate max semantics on pop). The list is bounded to K entries.\\n# Input value: dict {\\\"player_id\\\": str, \\\"score\\\": number}  (player's current max).\\n# Output value: list of dicts {\\\"player_id\\\": str, \\\"score\\\": number} sorted by\\n#               score descending — the current Top-K view.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nclass _TopKState:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Wrapper around a max-heap bounded to K entries.\\n\\n    We store negated scores in a min-heap so that ``heapq.heappop`` always\\n    removes the *smallest* entry (i.e. the player outside the Top-K).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    __slots__ = (\\\"_heap\\\", \\\"_k\\\")\\n\\n    def __init__(self, k: int):\\n        # Heap entries: (-score, player_id). The 0-th tuple element drives\\n        # the heap ordering, so negating the score produces a max-heap via\\n        # Python's stdlib min-heap.\\n        self._heap: List[Tuple[float, str]] = []\\n        self._k = k\\n\\n    def update(self, player_id: str, score: float) -> None:\\n        # Remove any prior entry for this player so stale tuples don't\\n        # linger in the heap.\\n        self._heap = [(s, p) for (s, p) in self._heap if p != player_id]\\n        heapq.heapify(self._heap)\\n        # Insert the new score and trim to K.\\n        heapq.heappush(self._heap, (-float(score), player_id))\\n        while len(self._heap) > self._k:\\n            heapq.heappop(self._heap)\\n\\n    def snapshot(self) -> List[dict]:\\n        # Build a sorted (descending by score) view of the current heap.\\n        ordered = sorted(self._heap, key=lambda sp: sp[0])  # ascending by -score\\n        return [\\n            {\\\"player_id\\\": p, \\\"score\\\": -s}\\n            for (s, p) in ordered\\n        ]\\n\\n\\ndef _make_track_top_k(k: int):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Build a stateful_map mapper closure that knows the Top-K size.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def track_top_k(\\n        state: Optional[_TopKState], event: dict\\n    ) -> Tuple[_TopKState, List[dict]]:\\n        if state is None:\\n            state = _TopKState(k)\\n        state.update(event[\\\"player_id\\\"], event[\\\"score\\\"])\\n        snapshot = state.snapshot()\\n        return (state, snapshot)\\n\\n    return track_top_k\\n\\n\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Custom sink that captures the very last emitted item — the final Top-K.\\n# Using a DynamicSink keeps everything inside the Bytewax execution model.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nclass _CaptureSinkPartition(StatelessSinkPartition):\\n    def __init__(self, owner: \\\"FinalCaptureSink\\\"):\\n        self._owner = owner\\n\\n    @override\\n    def write_batch(self, items: List[object]) -> None:\\n        # Items within a single batch arrive in order; we just want the\\n        # most-recent (i.e. last) one. After the dataflow completes, this\\n        # will be the final Top-K as it stood at EOF.\\n        if items:\\n            self._owner.value = items[-1]\\n\\n\\nclass FinalCaptureSink(DynamicSink[object]):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Sinks items into a Python attribute — ``value`` — for later use.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\\n        self.value: Optional[object] = None\\n\\n    @override\\n    def build(\\n        self, _step_id: str, _worker_index: int, _worker_count: int\\n    ) -> _CaptureSinkPartition:\\n        return _CaptureSinkPartition(self)\\n\\n\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Dataflow construction.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\ndef build_flow(k: int, sink: FinalCaptureSink) -> Dataflow:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Assemble the leaderboard dataflow.\\n\\n    The pipeline is:\\n\\n      FileSource (raw JSONL lines)\\n        -> map (JSON parse)\\n        -> key_on (player_id)\\n        -> stateful_map (per-player max score)\\n        -> map_value (drop the player_id from the value, keeping it in key)\\n        -> key_rm (recover (player_id, score) tuples)\\n        -> key_on (constant \\\"global\\\" key)  -- routes to single worker\\n        -> stateful_map (global Top-K maintenance via min-heap)\\n        -> output (FinalCaptureSink)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # ``input_path`` is supplied later via a closure; we read it from stdin\\n    # in the runtime harness via ``FileSource``. For building, we use a\\n    # placeholder filename because FileSource is lazy — the actual path is\\n    # patched in just before ``run_main`` is called.\\n    raise NotImplementedError(\\n        \\\"Use build_flow_for_path(path, k, sink) to construct the dataflow.\\\"\\n    )\\n\\n\\ndef build_flow_for_path(\\n    input_path: str, k: int, sink: FinalCaptureSink\\n) -> Dataflow:\\n    flow = Dataflow(\\\"leaderboard\\\")\\n    # 1. Read raw lines from the JSONL input file (lazy).\\n    lines = input(\\\"inp_lines\\\", flow, FileSource(input_path))\\n    # 2. Parse each line as JSON. Empty/blank lines are skipped.\\n    events = map(\\\"parse\\\", lines, _safe_json_loads)\\n    # 3. Key the stream by player_id so each player's score updates are\\n    #    routed to the same stateful worker.\\n    keyed = key_on(\\\"key_player\\\", events, lambda e: e[\\\"player_id\\\"])\\n    # 4. Per-player max-score tracking using stateful_map.\\n    max_scores: \\\"...\\\" = stateful_map(\\\"track_max\\\", keyed, track_max)\\n    # 5. Drop the player_id key off the stream so we can re-key to \\\"global\\\"\\n    #    while keeping the player_id information inside the value.\\n    pairs = max_scores.key_rm(\\\"rm_player_key\\\")\\n    # 6. Route every (player_id, score) pair to a single \\\"global\\\" key so\\n    #    a single stateful operator computes the global Top-K.\\n    global_stream = key_on(\\\"global_key\\\", pairs, lambda _item: \\\"global\\\")\\n    # 7. The emitted snapshot\\n# is the current Top-K sorted by score descending.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nclass _TopKState:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Wrapper around a max-heap bounded to K entries.\\n\\n    Storing ``(-score, player_id)`` in a min-heap lets us pop the smallest\\n    score (i.e. the player outside the Top-K) in O(log K) time and still\\n    produce a deterministic snapshot at any time.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    __slots__ = (\\\"_heap\\\", \\\"_k\\\")\\n\\n    def __init__(self, k: int) -> None:\\n        # Heap entries: (-score, player_id)\\n        self._heap: List[Tuple[float, str]] = []\\n        self._k = k\\n\\n    def update(self, player_id: str, score: float) -> None:\\n        # Drop any stale entry for this player to keep the heap compact.\\n        if self._heap:\\n            self._heap = [(s, p) for (s, p) in self._heap if p != player_id]\\n            heapq.heapify(self._heap)\\n        heapq.heappush(self._heap, (-float(score), player_id))\\n        # Trim the heap to K (the smallest scores fall out first).\\n        while len(self._heap) > self._k:\\n            heapq.heappop(self._heap)\\n\\n    def snapshot(self) -> List[dict]:\\n        # ``self._heap`` is unordered; ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Stateful mapper: track the highest score seen for each player.\\n#\\n# Input value: a dict {\\\"player_id\\\": str, \\\"score\\\": number}.\\n# State: float | None  (previous highest score for this player).\\n# Output value: a dict {\\\"player_id\\\": str, \\\"score\\\": <current max>} for\\n# downstream consumption.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\ndef track_max(state: Optional[float], event: dict) -> Tuple[float, dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Update the per-player max score and emit the current max.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    new_score = float(event[\\\"score\\\"])\\n    if state is None:\\n        new_max = new_score\\n    elif new_score > state:\\n negation on Python's min-heap)\\n# containing at most K entries of ``(-score, player_id)`` pairs. On each\\n# update, any prior entry for the same player is removed, the new entry is\\n# inserted, and the heap is trimmed to K. The emitted snapshot is the\\n# current Top-K sorted by score descending.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nclass _TopKState:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Wrapper around a max-heap bounded to ``K`` entries.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    __slots__ = (\\\"_heap\\\", \\\"_k\\\")\\n\\n    def __init__(self, k: int) -> None:\\n        # Heap entries: (-score, player_id). We do this so skip-items still produce a stream\\n    item (since we can't remove items from a Bytewax stream mid-flow). The\\n    downstream stateful_map picks the higher score; sentinels never win.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if event is None:\\n        return {\\\"player_id\\\": \\\"__SKIP__\\\", \\\"score\\\": float(\\\"-inf\\\")}\\n    return event\\n\\n\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Dataflow construction.\\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\ndef build_flow_for_path(\\n    input_path: str, k: int, sink: FinalCaptureSink\\n) -> Dataflow:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Construct the leaderboard dataflow for a given input file path.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    flow = Dataflow(\\\"leaderboard\\\")\\n\\n    # 1.
